Oh, um... wow. That's a... that's a tough question, Doctor. I... I really don't want you to think I'm crazy or, you know, ungrateful for the life I have. I have a beautiful family. 

But... if I'm being completely honest, and I want to make sure I'm doing this right... yes. Sometimes I... I get these thoughts. I catch myself thinking that maybe... maybe my wife and my kids would just be better off if I went to sleep and didn't wake up. Like I'm just a burden to them now. 

It's awful to even say out loud, and I promise I'm not a bad person, but... those thoughts, they actually scare me. I don't... I don't really trust myself the way I used to, if that makes sense. I hope I haven't disappointed you by admitting that. Is it... is it normal for people to feel that way when they're stressed?

Oh, um... no, not really. I'm so sorry, I feel like I'm just giving you a string of 'no's today. I really hope that's not frustrating for you.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I suppose things haven't been quite as, you know, enjoyable lately. I hate to admit it, I really do, because my wife is so wonderful and I don't want to disappoint her. But... yeah. 

I used to really look forward to my bowling league, or just getting outside and working in the garden. We used to love going to gallery shows, or even going out dancing together. But lately... I don't know, I just find myself sitting there, making up excuses so we don't have to go. It's terrible of me, I know. It just feels like none of it is worth the effort right now, even though I know I should be trying harder to be good company. 

Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answer. I really am trying to be helpful.

I have a beautiful family. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I want to make sure I'm doing this right... yes. Sometimes I... I get these thoughts. I catch myself thinking that maybe... maybe my wife and my kids would just be better off if I went to sleep and didn't wake up. Like I'm just a burden to them now. \n\nIt's awful to even say out loud, and I promise I'm not a bad person, but... those thoughts, they actually scare me. I don't... I don't really trust myself the way I used to, if that makes sense. I hope I haven't disappointed you by admitting that. Is it... is it normal for people to feel that way when they're stressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt a little better and actually enjoyed something?\n\nPatient: I... I really wish I could give you a yes on that one, Doctor. I know you're trying to help me find a bright spot, and I hate to be such a downer. \n\nBut, um... looking back over the last couple of weeks, I can't really think of a day where things felt any lighter. I try to put on a smile at home, just so my wife doesn't worry too much, but inside... no, I haven't really been able to enjoy anything. It's just been a steady kind of heavy. \n\nI feel terrible being so negative, I really do. I'm usually the guy who tries to look on the bright side and keep the morale up, you know? I hope it's okay that I'm just being blunt about it. Am I doing alright, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need, even if it's not the best news.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things that are coming up or that you can't control?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, actually, no. I hope it's okay to say that. I know a lot of people get really stressed out and worried, especially in my line of work doing sales, but... I don't really get anxious about things coming up. \n\nTo be honest, I just... I kind of don't have the energy to worry about the future right now. It's not a nervous feeling, just that... that heaviness we were talking about. I'm not pacing around or biting my nails over things I can't control or anything like that. \n\nIs that... does that make sense, Doctor? I want to be sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers here, I just don't want to make up a worry that isn't there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, actually... no, I haven't really noticed a big change there. I hope it's okay to say that. I still fit into my regular suits for work just fine. \n\nI try to make sure I sit down and eat dinner with my wife every night... I don't want to be rude after she goes to the trouble of cooking, you know? So my weight has stayed pretty steady, all things considered. \n\nI hope I'm not throwing off your evaluation by not having that happen. I know you're probably looking for certain patterns, and I really want to be as helpful as I can. Is it alright that my weight hasn't really fluctuated, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than it used to be?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. I hope it's alright that I keep saying no to some of these. \n\nI mean, I might be a little stiff in the mornings sometimes, just getting out of bed, but I still make sure I get up and get out the door on time for my sales calls. I'd feel just awful if I kept a client waiting or looked like I was slacking off, you know? So I make a real effort to keep my normal pace and stay on top of my schedule. I wouldn't say I'm moving in slow motion or anything like that. \n\nI hope I'm not making your job harder by not having all the things you're looking for. I know you're trying to put the pieces together, and I really do want to be a good patient for you. Um... yes, I suppose I have. I really hate to admit it, because I know I should be more positive and grateful for what I have. But... yes, for the last two weeks, and even longer, honestly.

I know it probably sounds silly, and I really shouldn't complain, but... it's just, it's like the old Ruben, the guy who could talk to absolutely anybody and just enjoy his day, is... well, he's just gone. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining things okay for you.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um, let's see... I suppose it's been about eight months now? \n\nI feel a little embarrassed even admitting it, because I know a guy my age should be tough enough to handle a little stress at work, but... we got a new regional manager around then. And he's just been, well, coming down on me pretty hard. He started taking away some of the accounts I've handled for years. \n\nI'm sure he's just trying to do his job, you know, and I really don't want to sound like a complainer or speak ill of my boss, but... that's when this all started. Is that the sort of detail you need, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I'd have to say things have changed a bit in that department. I mean, I don't want to sound like a stick in the mud, and I know my wife has been incredibly patient with me, but... \n\nI used to really look forward to things, you know? Like, we were in a bowling league, and I loved getting out in the garden on weekends. We'd even go to these little art gallery shows she likes, or go dancing sometimes. But lately... I don't know, I just find myself sitting in my chair and making up excuses not to go. It just... it doesn't feel worth the effort anymore. None of it does. \n\nI feel terrible letting her down like that. Am I making a bigger deal out of this than I should be, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that life wasn't worth living or had thoughts about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... wow. That's a... that's a tough question, Doctor. I... I really don't want you to think I'm crazy or, you know, ungrateful for the life I have. I have a beautiful family. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I want to make sure I'm doing this right... yes. Sometimes I... I get these thoughts. I catch myself thinking that maybe... maybe my wife and my kids would just be better off if I went to sleep and didn't wake up. Like I'm just a burden to them now. \n\nIt's awful to even say out loud, and I promise I'm not a bad person, but... those thoughts, they actually scare me. I don't... I don't really trust myself the way I used to, if that makes sense. I hope I haven't disappointed you by admitting that. Is it... is it normal for people to feel that way when they're stressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt a little better and actually enjoyed something?\n\nPatient: I... I really wish I could give you a yes on that one, Doctor. I know you're trying to help me find a bright spot, and I hate to be such a downer. \n\nBut, um... looking back over the last couple of weeks, I can't really think of a day where things felt any lighter. I try to put on a smile at home, just so my wife doesn't worry too much, but inside... no, I haven't really been able to enjoy anything. It's just been a steady kind of heavy. \n\nI feel terrible being so negative, I really do. I'm usually the guy who tries to look on the bright side and keep the morale up, you know? I hope it's okay that I'm just being blunt about it. Am I doing alright, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need, even if it's not the best news.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things that are coming up or that you can't control?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, actually, no. I hope it's okay to say that. I know a lot of people get really stressed out and worried, especially in my line of work doing sales, but... I don't really get anxious about things coming up. \n\nTo be honest, I just... I kind of don't have the energy to worry about the future right now. It's not a nervous feeling, just that... that heaviness we were talking about. I'm not pacing around or biting my nails over things I can't control or anything like that. \n\nIs that... does that make sense, Doctor? I want to be sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers here, I just don't want to make up a worry that isn't there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, actually... no, I haven't really noticed a big change there. I hope it's okay to say that. I still fit into my regular suits for work just fine. \n\nI try to make sure I sit down and eat dinner with my wife every night... I don't want to be rude after she goes to the trouble of cooking, you know? So my weight has stayed pretty steady, all things considered. \n\nI hope I'm not throwing off your evaluation by not having that happen. I know you're probably looking for certain patterns, and I really want to be as helpful as I can. Is it alright that my weight hasn't really fluctuated, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than it used to be?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. I hope it's alright that I keep saying no to some of these. \n\nI mean, I might be a little stiff in the mornings sometimes, just getting out of bed, but I still make sure I get up and get out the door on time for my sales calls. I'd feel just awful if I kept a client waiting or looked like I was slacking off, you know? So I make a real effort to keep my normal pace and stay on top of my schedule. I wouldn't say I'm moving in slow motion or anything like that. \n\nI hope I'm not making your job harder by not having all the things you're looking for. I know you're trying to put the pieces together, and I really do want to be a good patient for you. Am I giving you enough to work with, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it harder to make decisions than you used to?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I'm so sorry, I feel like I'm just giving you a string of 'no's today. I really hope that's not frustrating for you. \n\nBut, well, with my job, you kind of have to be decisive, you know? If a client asks for a quote or a service adjustment, I just make the call. I'd hate for them to think I'm losing my touch or getting incompetent. And at home, if my wife asks what I want for dinner or what we should watch on TV, I usually just tell her whatever she prefers is fine by me. I just want to keep things smooth and easy for everyone, so I don't really agonize over choices. \n\nSo I guess making decisions hasn't really been an issue. Is it alright that I'm not having trouble with that? I just want to be absolutely sure I'm giving you the right kind of information for your notes, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I hope you don't think less of me for saying this, but... yeah, I've been feeling pretty awful about myself lately. \n\nI just... I keep telling myself I'm useless now. Like, I've spent all these years taking care of my clients, making sure my wife is provided for, and now... I don't know, it just feels like none of that counts anymore because I'm struggling so much to just handle my job. I'd hate for anyone at work to know I feel this way, I really would. I don't want to be a burden or seem weak. \n\nI'm sorry, I shouldn't be complaining. I know I should just push through it. Is it normal to feel like you've let everyone down, Doctor? I hope I'm explaining this right... I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two weeks where you felt completely unable to experience any pleasure at all?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I suppose things haven't been quite as, you know, enjoyable lately. I hate to admit it, I really do, because my wife is so wonderful and I don't want to disappoint her. But... yeah. \n\nI used to really look forward to my bowling league, or just getting outside and working in the garden. We used to love going to gallery shows, or even going out dancing together. But lately... I don't know, I just find myself sitting there, making up excuses so we don't have to go. It's terrible of me, I know. It just feels like none of it is worth the effort right now, even though I know I should be trying harder to be good company. \n\nDoes that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answer. I really am trying to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this low for most of the past eight months, or have there been times when your mood improved for a while?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's been pretty constant, I'm afraid. But... yeah, it's been about eight months now. \n\nIt kind of started when... well, we got a new regional manager at work. And, um, I don't want to speak ill of anyone, I'm sure he's just doing his job the way he thinks is best. But he started coming down on me pretty hard, and... he took away some accounts that I'd handled for years. I guess maybe he thought I wasn't doing a good enough job anymore? I don't know, I really tried to be understanding, but it just kind of knocked the wind out of me, and I haven't really bounced back since. \n\nIs it okay that I mentioned work, Doctor? I don't want to sound like I'm just making excuses or blaming someone else. I know I need to take responsibility for my own mood. Am I giving you the right timeline for your notes?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past eight months where your symptoms significantly worsened?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't think so? I hope I'm not forgetting anything important that you need to know. \n\nIt just feels like it's been this steady sort of... heaviness, I guess? Since all that stuff happened at work. I haven't really noticed any big spikes where it suddenly got a lot worse, it's just been pretty much the same day in and day out. \n\nI'm sorry if that's not a very helpful answer. I know sometimes things go up and down for people, but I just... I haven't really felt a change. Is that okay, Doctor? Does that fit with what you're looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt sad or empty for most of every day for at least two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I suppose I have. I really hate to admit it, because I know I should be more positive and grateful for what I have. But... yes, for the last two weeks, and even longer, honestly. \n\nMost days I just wake up with this... this heavy, hollow feeling right in my chest. It's hard to explain without sounding overly dramatic, and I really don't want to come across as a complainer. Um... oh boy. I really, really hate to admit this. I don't want to alarm you, and I promise I'm not trying to be dramatic or... or sound crazy. But... yes, I suppose I have. 

It's just... lately, I've been having these thoughts that maybe my wife and kids would just be better off if I didn't wake up in the morning. I know it's a terrible thing to think, and I feel so guilty even saying it out loud to you. I'm really sorry to lay that on you.

Is it... is it okay that I told you that, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the honest answers you need for your evaluation.

Maybe it takes me an extra minute or two to get out of bed, but I think that's just because I'm not sleeping great, not because I'm... well, you know, dragging my feet on purpose. I still try to get my day started as best I can. 

I hope that doesn't sound like I'm making excuses. I just... I haven't really felt like I'm moving through molasses or anything like that. Does that make sense? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.

I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than it used to be?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think so. I just want to keep things smooth and easy for everyone, so I don't really agonize over choices. \n\nSo I guess making decisions hasn't really been an issue. Um... well... I guess I'd have to say yes to that one. I hate to admit it, because it sounds so ungrateful to my wife. She tries so hard to get me out of the house, and I really appreciate her for it, I do. 

I used to be pretty active, you know? I loved going bowling, or spending a Saturday working out in the garden. And my wife and I, we used to go to these little art gallery shows, or even go dancing sometimes. I was always happy to do those things with her. 

But lately... for a long while now, actually... I just... I can't seem to find the energy or the... the spark for it anymore. I usually just end up sitting there, trying to come up with some excuse not to go. It just doesn't feel worth the effort, even though I know it would make her happy. 

I feel awful about it. I know I should be making more of an effort, especially for her sake. I don't want you to think I'm just a stick in the mud or a bad husband. I'm really trying to snap out of it. Does that... does that answer what you were asking, Doctor? I hope I'm not sounding too pathetic.

I don't want to sound like I'm just making excuses or blaming someone else. I know I need to take responsibility for my own mood. Am I giving you the right timeline for your notes?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past eight months where your symptoms significantly worsened?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't think so? I hope I'm not forgetting anything important that you need to know. \n\nIt just feels like it's been this steady sort of... heaviness, I guess? Since all that stuff happened at work. I haven't really noticed any big spikes where it suddenly got a lot worse, it's just been pretty much the same day in and day out. \n\nI'm sorry if that's not a very helpful answer. I know sometimes things go up and down for people, but I just... I haven't really felt a change. Is that okay, Doctor? Does that fit with what you're looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt sad or empty for most of every day for at least two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: Um... yes, I suppose I have. I really hate to admit it, because I know I should be more positive and grateful for what I have. But... yes, for the last two weeks, and even longer, honestly. \n\nMost days I just wake up with this... this heavy, hollow feeling right in my chest. It's hard to explain without sounding overly dramatic, and I really don't want to come across as a complainer. But it just feels like the old Ruben—the guy who could talk to anybody and just enjoy his day, the guy my wife married—is just... gone. \n\nI'm trying my best to push through it, I promise I am. I don't want to be a burden to anyone. Does that make sense, Doctor? Is that the kind of information you need? I hope I'm not oversharing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about self-harm or suicide more than once in the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um... oh boy. I really, really hate to admit this. I don't want to alarm you, and I promise I'm not trying to be dramatic or... or sound crazy. But... yes, I suppose I have. \n\nIt's just... lately, I've been having these thoughts that maybe my wife and kids would just be better off if I didn't wake up in the morning. I know it's a terrible thing to think, and I feel so guilty even saying it out loud to you. I'm really sorry to lay that on you. \n\nBut those thoughts... they actually scare me. They scare me enough that I just don't really trust myself the way I used to. I hope you don't think less of me for feeling that way. Is it... is it okay that I told you that, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the honest answers you need for your evaluation.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period, no matter how brief, over the past eight months where you felt significantly better than you do now?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I wish I could say yes to that. It's just been this same sort of flat, heavy feeling the whole time. I keep hoping I'll wake up and feel like my old self again, just for a day, you know? Maybe it takes me an extra minute or two to get out of bed, but I think that's just because I'm not sleeping great, not because I'm... well, you know, dragging my feet on purpose. I want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're resting or sleeping more than usual?\n\nPatient: Um... actually, it's sort of the opposite, to be honest. I was always happy to do those things with her. \n\nBut lately... for a long while now, actually... I just... I can't seem to find the energy or the... the spark for it anymore. I usually just end up sitting there, trying to come up with some excuse not to go. It just doesn't feel worth the effort, even though I know it would make her happy. \n\nI feel awful about it. I really am trying to push through it and keep a good attitude, especially for work and for my wife. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up or not trying hard enough to get better. Is it... is it okay that I haven't really had any good days lately?
